CLEAR WEAPON before processing. Do not actuate toggles until weapon has been cleared.
Inspect chamber to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in position
to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of preservation area.

Step                                              Function                               Mech  Insp

      a. Unit Responsibility.
1 | Assure that bayonets and scabbards are in good condition.
2 | Deliver bayonets and scabbards to designated processing area.
3 | Unit Commander or representative will remain with equipment until acceptance

          by responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

1 | Disassemble scabbard and bayonet as necessary to accomplish desired cleaning.

2 Clean all metallic surfaces by wiping with rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570)

       saturated with dry-cleaning solvent (P--D-680) (6850~-285-8012).

3 | Drain and dry with compressed air or wiping rag (DDD-—R-30) (7920-205-3570).

4 | Fabricate a fiberboard box, (PPP-F-320) (8135-132-9067), 22” x 18/7 x 11”.

5 | Cushioning material shall be located in bottom of container (PPP-C-843) (8135-

       855-6969) 13’ x 227’.

6 | Locate paper, volatile packaging (MIL-—P-3420) (8135-985~7242) 13’’ x 22” on

       top of cushioning.

7 | Place ten (10) bayonets with scabbards on layer of VCI alternating the bayonet

       handles end to end.

8 | Repeat the above operation for fifty (50) bayonets with scabbards.

9 | Material needed for 50 bayonets, 6 pieces of cushioning (PPP—C-843) (8135-855-

       6969), size 13’’ x 22”’ and 5 pieces of (MIL-P-3420) (8135-985-7242), 13’ x 22’,

       distributed as outlined in steps 5, 6 and 7.

10 | Close and seal all seams and manufacturer’s joints of fiberboard box with pressure

       sensitive tape (PPP-T-60) (8135-890-9873) 2”” x 131”.

11 Apply the following information on each fiberboard container with stencil ink

                            FSN                                       ITEM DESCRIPTION
            [] 1005-072-5011     Rifle, 7.62 Millimeter, M14A1, W/E.

      PRESERVED:                             _—__

                                 (Month)              (Day)                             (Year)

      PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

-     NOTE:

            Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

            Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel. to indicate function has been

.                  completed.

            Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

                   or processing personnel.

      Step                                   Function                                             Mech  Insp

      ee    a. Unit Responsibility.
            Assure that weapon is in operating condition prior to movement to processing site.
   4        Certify that weapon, magazines and containers are free of ammunition.
            Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment, to designated processing site.
      PWN   Unit commander or representative will remain with weapons until acceptance by re-

      od i      sponsible preservation site personnel.

            b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

                                                                                       WARNING

                        Before cleaning and packaging, be sure weapon is clear. Do not actuate
                        trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to insure that
                        it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in position to be
                        introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of work area.

            Remove stabilizer, magazine and bipod.
            Disassemble weapon, as necessary in preparation for thorough cleaning.
            Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with a clean rag, (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-

                3570) saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806).
            Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior surfaces with a snug

                fitting bristle brushes (1005-556-4174) and (1005-690-8441), saturated with sol-
                vent cleaning compound, (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished as
                “special tools’’.
            Further cleaning of metal part shall be accomplished by complete immersion in dry-
                cleaning solvent, (P—D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and
                agitating or with a solvent-saturated rag, (DDD-—R-30) (7920-205-3570).
            Rinse parts with clean dry-cleaning solvent, (P-D~680) (6850-285-8012).
            Drain and dry clean parts with dry compressed air or with wiping rags, (DDD-R-30)
                 (7920—205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried with cleaning
                swabs (1005-288-3565). All cleaned surfaces shall be dried prior to preservative
                application.
            Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials, and wood shall be by any process
                or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
                damage to the item(s) (brushing, wiping, etc.).

Step                                          Function                                         Mech  Insp

9 | Apply general purpose lubricating oil, (VV—-L-800) 9150-281-2007, to all metallic
           surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be drained prior to packag-

       ing.

10 | Insert a VCI treated bore tube, (1005-336-0225), into bore of barrel bending pro-

       truding portion over the muzzle end.

11 | Fold sling and wrap with a piece of volatile paper (MIL—P-3420) (8135-985-7242),

       8’ x 12".

12 | Wrap weapon with a piece of volatile paper (MIL-P~3420) (8135-985-7242), 18’

       x 487’.

13 | Insert weapon and sling, minus stabilizer and bipod, into a barrier bag (MIL-B-131)

       (8135-282-0565), 12’ x 50”.

14 | Close bag by heat sealing.

15 | Wrap stabilizer and bipod in volatile paper (MIL—P-3420) (8135-985-7242), 18”’ x

       x 367’.

16 | Overwrap stabilizer and bipod in cushioning material (PPP-C-843) (8135-855~-6969),

       18’ x 36” (8 pieces required).

17 | Secure cushioning material with pressure sensitive tape (PPP-T--60) (8135-890-9873),

       2’’ x 6’’ (4 pieces required).

       10” x 18”,

19 | Close bag by heat sealing.

20 | Wrap each bagged weapon with cushioning material (PPP-C-843) (8135-855-6969),

       18’’ x 48”" (8 pieces required).

21 | Secure in place with pressure sensitive tape (PPP-T-60) (8135-890-9873), 2’’ x 6”’

       (4 pieces required).

22 | Position four (4) bag cushioned weapons alternately in a corrugated fiberboard box

       (PPP--F-320) (8135-132-9067), 454" x 214" x 5K”.

23 | Cover weapons with cushioning material (PPP-C-843) (8135~-855-6969), 22’ x 46”’.

       in container.

28 | Place items 1 through 7 (table A~2-2) in a barrier bag (MIL-B~131) (8135-282-

       0565), 10’’ x 12”.

29 | Wrap magazines, item 8 (table A~-2-2) in volatile paper (MIL—P-3420) (8135-985—

       7242), 9’’ x 9’’ (5 pieces required).

30 | Insert magazines in a barrier bag (MIL-—B-131) (8135-282-0565), 10’’ x 12’.

31 | Close bag by heat sealing.

32 | Position four (4) sets of bagged bipods and four (4) sets of Basic Issue Items into

       container.

33°] Cover bagged items with cushioning material (PPP—C-843) (8135-855-6969), 24’” x

       48”,

34 | Close box and seal with pressure sensitive tape (PPP-T--60) (8135-890-9873),

       2’’ x 48”’ (2 pieces required).

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                   Function                                             Mech  Insp

N     a, Unit Responsibility.
      Assure that weapon is in operating condition prior to movement to processing site.
R w   Certify that weapon, magazine and containers are free of ammunition.
      Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment, to designated processing site.
       Unit commander or representative will remain with weapons until acceptance byor

          responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                        WARNING

                   Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                    Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                   to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                   position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity
                   of work area.

1 | Disassemble weapon as necessary in preparation for thorough cleaning.
2 | Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with cleaning rags (DDD~R-30) (7920-205-

          3570), saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL—C-372) (6850-753-4806).
3 | Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior surfaces with brush

            (1005-903-1296), saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372)
           (6850~—753-4806). Use rod furnished as “special tools.”
4 | Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in dry-
           cleaning solvent (P—D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and
           agitation or with a solvent saturated cleaning rag (DDD-—R-30) (7920-205-3570).
       Rinse parts with clean drycleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
6 | Drain and dry cleaned parts with dry compressed air, or with wiping rags (DDD-R-
           30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be thoroughly dried
           prior to preservative application.
7 | Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials, and wood shall be by any process
           or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
            damage to the item(s) (brushing, wiping, etc.).

10 | Insert a bore tube volatile corrosion inhibitor (1005-336—-0216) into barrel bore

iw)   a. Unit Responsibility.
      Assure that weapon is in good operating condition.
OH    Certify that weapon and containers are free of ammunition and explosives.
      As directed, weapon complete with tools and equipment, will be delivered to desig-
ono
         nated processing site.
      Unit Commander, or representative will remain with equipment until acceptance by

         responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

                                                                       WARNING

                  Before cleaning and packaging Be Sure Weapon Is Clear. Do not actu-
                  ate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to insure
                  that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in position to
                 be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of work area.

      Disassemble weapon as necessary to accomplish thorough cleaning.
      Clean barrel bore, chamber, and any parts having heavy contaminates with a metallic

          brush (1005-903~-1296) saturated with solvent, cleaning compound (MIL-C-372)
          (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished as ‘‘special tools.”
      Remove cleaner from bore and chamber using swab, small arms, size 24’ x 2’
          (1005-288-3565).
      Further cleaning of all surfaces shall be accomplished by complete immersion, or, by
          wiping with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570), saturated with solvent (P-D-
          680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and agitation cleaning.
      Position parts to allow complete drainage.
      Rinse parts in clean solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), to remove residues.
      Drain and dry with compressed air, or wipe with rag. (DDD-—R-30) (7920-205-3570).
      Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials, and wood, shall be by any process,
          or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
          damage to the item(s). Clean by brushing, wiping, etc.
      Reassemble weapon.
      Metallic surfaces of weapons shall be coated with corrosion preventative compound
          (VV-L-800) (9150-281~2007), applied by dipping.

Ne     a. Unit Responsibility.
       Assure that weapon is in good operating condition.
       Certify that weapon and container’s are free of ammunition and explosives.
3 | As directed, weapon, complete with tools and equipment, will be delivered to desig-

           nated processing site.
4 | Unit Commander, or representative will remain with equipment until acceptance by

           responsible preservation site personnel.

       b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

                                                                        WARNING

                   Before cleaning and packaging, Be Sure Weapon is Clear. Do not
                    actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to insure
                   that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in position to be
                  introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of work area.

       Disassemble weapon as necessary to accomplish thorough cleaning.
2 | Clean barrel bore, chambers and any parts having heavy contaminates with a

           metallic brush (1005-903-1296) saturated with solvent, cleaning (M1L~C-372)
           (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished as “special tools’’.
3 | Remove cleaner from bore and chambers using swabs, size 2%’’ x 2%’ (1005-288-
           3565).
4 | Further cleaning of oil surfaces shall be accomplished by complete immersion, or
           by wiping with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570), saturated with solvent
            (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012) accomplished by scrubbing and agitation.
5 | Position parts to allow complete drainage.
6 | Rinse parts in clean solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285~8012), to remove residues.
7 | Drain, and dry with compressed air, or wipe with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-
           3570).
8 | Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials and wood shall be by any process,
           or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
           damage to the item(s). Clean by brushing, wiping, etc.
9 | Reassemble weapon.

       L] 1005-840-3758                  Rifle, Caliber .22, Remington, Model 513T, W/E.

PRESERVED:                               oe

                                (Month)           (Day)  (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

       Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

       Mech:  For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

              completed.

       Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

              or processing personnel.

Step                                     Function                                                 Mech  Insp

ee     a. Unit Responsibility.
       Assure that weapon is in operating condition prior to movement to processing site.
Be WN  Certify that weapon, magazine and containers are free of ammunition.
       Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment to designated processing site.
—      Unit Commander, or representative, will remain with weapons until acceptance by

or         responsible preservation site personnel.

       b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                        WARNING

                   Before cleaning and packaging, Be Sure Weapon Is Clear. Do not actu-
                   ate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to insure
                   that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in position to
                   be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of work area.

       Disassemble weapon, as necessary, in preparation for thorough cleaning.
       Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with cleaning rags (DDD-—R-30) (7920—205-

          3570), saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MI1L-—C-372) (6850-753~4806).

       Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior metal surfaces with
           a snug fitting bristle brush (1005-903-1296), and swabs (1005-288-3565), saturated
           with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753~4806). Use rod fur-
           nished as “special tools”.

       Further cleaning of parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in dry clean-
           ing solvent (P—D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and agitation
           or with a solvent saturated cleaning rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570).

       Rinse parts with clean dry cleaning solvent (P~D-680) (6850-285-—8012).
       Drain and dry clean metal parts with dry compressed air or with wiping rags (DDD-

           R-30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be thoroughly dried
           prior to preservative application.
       Cleaning of canvas, webbing synthetic materials and wood shall be by any process
           or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
           damage to the item(s) (brushing, wiping, etc.).
       Apply general purpose lubricating oil (VV—-L-800) (9150-281-2007), to all metallic
           surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be drained prior to packaging.
       Reassemble weapon.

             PREPARATION {INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:
                           SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                           FSN                                                ITEM DESCRIPTION

      CJ 1005-317-2474                   Pistol, Automatic, Cal .22 High Standard Supermatic.

PRESERVED:

                                (Month)  (Day)     (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                     Function                                               Mech  Insp

        a. Unit Responsibility.
 1 | Assure that weapon and equipment are in good operating condition.
 2 | Certify that weapon, magazines, and containers are free of ammunition and explosives.
 3 | As directed, weapon complete with tools and equipment, will be delivered to desig-

            nated processing site.
 4 | Unit Commander, or representative will remain with equipment until acceptance

            by responsible preservation site personnel.

        b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

                                                                        WARNING

                     Before cleaning and packaging. BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                     Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                     to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                    position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity
                    of work area.

 1 | Disassemble weapons as necessary to accomplish thorough cleaning.
 2 Clean barrel bore, chamber, and any parts having heavy contaminants with a

             metallic brush (1005~-903-1296) saturated with solvent cleaning compound
            (MIL-—C-372) (6850~753~-4806). Use rod issued with weapon.
 3 | Remove cleaner from bore using swab, small arms cleaning, size 2%’ x 24%’ (1005-
             288-3565).
 4 | Further cleaning of all surfaces shall be accomplished by complete immersion, or,
            by wiping with rags (DDD~R-30) (7920-205-3570) saturated with solvent, dry
             cleaning (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012) accompanied by scrubbing and agitation.
 5 | Position parts to allow complete drainage.
 6 | Rinse parts in clean solvent, dry cleaning (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), to remove
             residues.
 7 | Drain and dry with compressed air, or, wipe with rags (DDD-—R-30) (7920-205-3570).
 8 | Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials, and wood, shall be by any process,
             or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
            damage to the item(s). Clean by brushing, wiping, etc.
 9 | Reassemble weapon.
10 | Metallic surfaces of weapons shall be coated with corrosion preventative compound,
            lubricating oil, general purpose (VV-L-800) (9150-281-2007).

                                                                                                      A-8-1

TM 750-136

Step                                          Function                                         Mech  Insp

11 | Drain excess oil from weapon, changing draining position as necessary to accomplish

             thorough drainage.
12 | Wrap pistol with a 16’’ x 18’’ piece of paper, volatile, packaging (MIL~P-3420)

             (8135-985-7242).

13 | Place each wrapped pistol in a 7’’ x 15’’ bag (MIL-B-131) (8135-282-0565).

14 | Disassemble rod, cleaning (1005-716-2231) prior to packaging.

15 | Clean rod by immersion in solvent, drycleaning (P-D-680) (6850~285-8012),

             accompanied by scrubbing or agitation.
16 | Completely drain weapon of cleaning solvent prior to preservative application.

17 | Rod, cleaning, shall be coated by dipping in corrosion preventative compound,                         rd
            lubricating oil, general purpose (VV-L-800) (9150-281-2007).

18 | Completely drain of excess preservative oil prior to wrapping.

19 | Wrap rod with a 4’’ x 10” piece of paper, volatile, packaging (MIL-P-3420) (8135-

       985-7242).

20 | Place wrapped rod, cleaning, in bag with pistol.

21 | Close bag by heat sealing.

22 | Place sealed bag in a corrugated fiberboard box (PPP-F-320) (8135-132-9067),

       114 x 54’ x 24”.

23 | Fill void space of box with cushioning material, cellulosic (PPP—C-843) (8135-855-

       6969), size 12’7 x 12”’ x 4”.

24 | Seal the center seams of top and bottom pressure sensitive (PPP~T-60) (8135~890-

       9873), size 2’ x 51”.

25 | Apply the following marking on each unit container with ink, marking stencil, black
            TT-I-559, (7510-161-0811):

       1005-3 17-2474

       Pistol, Automatic, Cal .22

       1 Each

       Preserved on (month-day-year) for a maximum of 180 days

       Gross Weight: 3.34        Cube: .107

26 | Assemble cleated plywood shipping container, size 36’’ x 29%/’ x 21\’’, in accordance

             with Figure A-8 using 8D nails (FF-N-105) (5315-010-6507), 6D nails (FF-N—
             105) (5315-161-8607) and 10D nails (FF-N-105) (5315-010-6509).
27 | Place 120 pistols w/rods, unit packaged as above, in a shipping container, Figure A-8.

28 | Close shipping container with 8D nails (FF-N-105) (5315-010-6507).

29 | Secure shipping container with steel strapping (QQ-S-781) (8135-283-0669) size

       %4’’ x 120’’ long, 3 pieces required.

30 | Secure steel strapping with seals, %’’ (8125-239~-5293), 3 required.

31 | Apply the following marking on each shipping container with 1/’ minimum size

       marking, using ink marking, stencil, black (TT-I-559) (7510—161—0811):

       1005~317-2474

       Pistol, Automatic, Cal. .22

       120 Hach

       Preserved on (month-day-year) for a maximum of 180 days

       Gross Weight: 507         Cube: 18.94

32 | Preparation instructions sheets shall be completed and placed in a waterproof

       envelope (MIL-B-131) (8135-282-0565), size 10’’ x 12’’ and secured to the item/

       container in a conspicuous location.

      c. Deprocessing.
      Prior to placing this item in service, deprocessing action will be in reverse of check-

      L] 1005-322-9715                  Machine Gun, Caliber .50 Browning M2, Heavy Barrel Flexible W/E.

PRESERVED:                                        ;

                               (Month)  (Day)                     (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personne! to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                    Function                                             Mech           Insp

ne     a. Unit Responsibility.
       Assures that weapon and equipment are in good operating condition.
P w    Certify that weapon is free of ammunition in gun barrels, magazines and containers.
       Deliver complete weapon, with tools and equipment, to designated processing area.
       Unit commander, or representative, will remain with equipment until acceptance byoo

           responsible preservation site personnel.

       b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                         WARNING

                   Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                    Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                    to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                   position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity
                   of work area.

        Disassemble weapon ‘and BII equipment as necessary to accomplish thorough
            cleaning.

       Clean all metallic surfaces by wiping with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570)
            saturated with (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806) cleaning compound solvent.

       Clean both barrels, bore and chamber with swabs (1005~288~3565) using (MIL-C-
            372) (6850~753-4806).

        Clean all surfaces of machine gun having burned powder residue with a metallic
            bristle brush (1005-550-4037) saturated with rifle bore cleaner (M1L~-C-372)
             (6850—-753-4806). Use rod furnished as “special tools’’.

        After cleaning with (M1L-C-372) (6850-753-4806), rinse with a dry cleaning solvent
             (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).

        Drain and dry with compressed air or wiping rags (DDD-R-30) (7920~205-3570)
             changing drying position as necessary to accomplish thorough drainage.

        Dry both barrel bores and chambers with swabs (1005-288-3565).
        All cleaned surfaces shall be thoroughly dried prior to preservation.
        Preserve all machine gun surfaces and both barrels with preservative oil (VV—L-800)

             (9150-281-2007).
10 Completely drain off excess oil prior to reassembly and packaging.

[o2)  Unit Commander, or representative, will remain with weapons until acceptance by

          responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                       WARNING

                 Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR. Do
                  not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to
                  insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in posi-
                  tion to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
                  work area,

      Disassemble weapon, as necessary, to accomplish thorough cleaning.
      Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with cleaning rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205—

          3570) saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL~—C-372) (6050—753-4806).
      Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior metal with snug

          fitting bristle brush (1005—556-4174) and swabs (1005-288-3565), saturated with
          solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished as
          “special tools’’.
      Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in dry-
          cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012) accompanied by scrubbing and
          agitation or with a solvent-saturated cleaning rag (DDD-R-0) (7920—205-3570).
      Rinse metal parts with clean dry-cleaning solvent (P—D-680) (6850-285-8012).
      Drain and dry cleaned parts with dry, compressed air or with wiping rags (DDD-R-
          30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be thoroughly dried
          prior to preservative application.
      Apply general purpose lubricating oil (VV—-L-800) (9150-281-2007), to all metallic
          surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be drained prior to packaging.
      Reassemble weapon.
      Insert a VCI treated bore tube (1005-336-0225) into bore of barrel bending pro-
           truding portion over the muzzle end.

                                                         APPENDIX A-15

                               PREPARATION INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:
                                             SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                          FSN                        ITEM DESCRIPTION

      C) 1005-589-1271                  Rifle, 7.62 Millimeter, M14,W/E.

PRESERVED:                     (Month)  (Day)                             (Year)

PRESERVED          FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS
NOTE:
                   Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.
        Function:  For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been
       Mech:       completed.

       Insp:       For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

                   or processing personnel.

Step                                    Function                                             Mech  Insp

N ee  a. Unit Responsibility.
      Assure that weapon is in operating condition prior to movement of processing site.
mPa   Certify that weapon, magazines and containers are free of ammunition.
      Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment, to designated processing site.
—     Unit Commander or representative will remain with weapons until acceptance by

          responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

                                                                    WARNING

                  Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                  Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                  to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                  position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity
                  of work area.

      Disassemble weapon, as necessary, in preparation for thorough cleaning.
      Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with a rag (DDD~-R-30) (7920-205-3570),

          saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-~372) (6850-753-4806).
      Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior surfaces with a snug

          fitting bristle brush (1005-556-4174) and (1005-690-8441), saturated with solvent
          cleaning compound (MIL—C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished as “‘special
          tools.”
      Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in
          drycleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and
          agitating or with a solvent-saturated rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205~3570).
      Rinse parts with drycleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
      Drain and dry clean parts with dry compressed air or with dry wiping rags (DDD-
          R-30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried with cleaning
          swabs (1005—288-3565). All cleaned surfaces shall be dried prior to preservative
          application.
      Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials, and wood shall be by any process
          or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
          damage to the item(s) (brushing, wiping, etc.).

        surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be thoroughly drained prior
        to packaging.

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.
      Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

Step                                   Function                                             Mech  Insp

        a. Unit Responsibility.
 1 | Assure that weapon is in good operating condition.
 2 | Certify that weapon, magazine, and containers are free of ammunition and explosives.
 3 | As directed, weapon, complete with tools and equipment, will be delivered to desig-

            nated processing site.
 4 } Unit Commander, or representative, will remain with equipment until acceptance by

            responsible preservation site personnel.

        b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                       WARNING
                    Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                    Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                    to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                    position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
                     work area.

 1 | Disassemble weapon as necessary to accomplish thorough cleaning.
 2) Clean barrel bore, chamber and any parts having heavy contaminates with a metallic

            brush (1005-903-1296) saturated with solvent, cleaning (MIL-C-372) (6850-
            753-4806). Use rod furnished as “‘special tools.”
 3 | Remove cleaner from bore, using swabs, size 24’’ x 2%’’ (1005-288-3565).
 4 | Further cleaning of all surfaces shall be accomplished by complete immersion, or by
             wiping with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570) saturated with solvent (P-D-
            680) (6850-285-8012) accomplished by scrubbing and agitation.
 5 | Position parts to allow complete drainage.
 6 | Rinse parts in clean solvent (P- D-680) (6850-285-8012), to remove residues.
 7 | Drain and dry with compressed air, or wipe with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-
            3570).
 8 | Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials and wood shall be by any process,
             or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
            damage to the item(s). Clean by brushing, wiping, etc.
 9 | Reassemble weapon.
10 | Metallic surfaces, of weapons, shall be coated with corrosion preventative compound
            (VV-L-800) (9150-281-2007) applied by dipping.

                         Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR. Do
                           not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to

             Disassemble weapon as necessary to accomplish desired cleaning.
       2 | Clean all metallic surfaces by wiping with rag (DDD~R-30) (7920-205-3570) satu-

                  rated with (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806).
       3 | Cleaning barrel bore and chamber with swabs (1005-288-3565) using (MIL~C-372)

                  (6850~-753-4806).
       4 | Clean surfaces of machine gun parts having burned powder residue with a metallic

                  brush (1005~550-4037) saturated with rifle bore cleaner (MIL—C-372) (6850—753-
                  4806). Use rod furnished as “special tools’.
       5 | After cleaning with (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806) rinse with a dry cleaning solvent
                  (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
       6 | Drain and dry with compressed air or wiping rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570)
                  changing drying position as necessary to accomplish thorough drainage.
              Dry barrel bore and chamber with swabs (1005-288-3565).
              All cleaned surfaces shall be thoroughly dried prior to preservations.
              Preserve all metallic surfaces with preservative oil (VV—L-800) (9150-281-2007).
              Completely drain off excess oil prior to reassembly and packaging.
              Disengaged gun barrel shall be packaged separate from machine gun.

                         FSN                           ITEM DESCRIPTION
      [1 1005~605-7710                 Machine Gun, 7.62MM: M60, W/E.

PRESERVED:

                              (Month)  (Day)                             (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech:  For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic
             or processing personnel.

Step                                   Function                                                    Mech  Insp

Ne      a. Unit Responsibility.
        Assure that weapon is in operating condition prior to movement to processing site.
m W     Certify that weapon and containers are free of ammunition.
        Deliver weapon to designated processing site.
oo       Unit Commanders or representative will remain with weapon until acceptance by

             responsible preservation site personnel.
        b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

                                                                          WARNING

                    Before cleaning and packaging, Be Sure Weapon Is Clear. Do not ac-
                     tuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to insure
                     that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in position to be
                     introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of work area.

        Disassemble weapon as necessary in preparation for cleaning.
        Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with a rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570),

             saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806).
        Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior metal surfaces

             with snug fitting bristle brushes (1005-556-4174) and (1005-690-3115), satu-
             rated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-~372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod
            furnished as “‘special tools’’.
        Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in
            dry cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing
            and agitation or with a solvent saturated wiping rag (DDD-R~30) (7920-205-
            3570).
        Rinse parts with clean dry-cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850~285-8012).
        Drain and dry cleaned parts with dry compressed air or with wiping rags (DDD-
            R-30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried with clean-
            ing swabs (1005-288-3565). All cleaned surfaces shall be dried prior to preserva-
            tion application.
        Apply general purpose lubricating oil (VV—L-800) (9150-281-2007), to all metallic
            surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be drained prior to packaging.
        Reassemble weapon with the exception of the barrel assembly.
        Wrap weapon with a piece of volatile paper (MIL-P-3420) (8135-985-7242),
      i 24/" x 36”,

                                     APPENDIX A-19

             PREPARATION INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:
                          SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                       FSN                                          ITEM DESCRIPTION

      [] 1005-670-7670               Carbine, Caliber .30, M1, W/E.
      [] 1005-670~-7675              Carbine, Caliber .30, M2, W/E.

PRESERVED:

                            (Month)    (Day)                                          (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                   Function                                               Mech  Insp

Pwd   a. Unit Responsibility.
      Assure that the weapon and equipment are in operating condition prior to movement

          to processing site.

      Certify that weapon, magazines and containers are free of ammunition.
      Deliver weapon, completed with tools and equipment to designated processing site.
      Unit Commander or representative will remain with weapons until acceptance by

          responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                    WARNING

                Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of

                  work area.

      Disassemble weapon as necessary in preparation for cleaning.
      Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with a cleaning rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-

         3570), saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753--4806).
      Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior surfaces with a snug

         fitting metallic bristle brushes (1005-556-4174) and (1005-690-8441) saturated
         with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished

          as “special tools’’.

      Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in dry-
         cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and
         agitating or with a solvent saturated cleaning rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570).

      Rinse parts with clean dry-cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
      Drain and dry cleaned parts with dry compressed air or with wiping rags (DDD-R-

         30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried with cleaning
         swabs (1005-288-3565). All cleaned surfaces shall be dried prior to preservative

          application.

      Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials, and wood shall be by any process or
          combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without damage

           to the item(s) (brushing, wiping, etc.).

                                                        APPENDIX A-20

                              PREPARATION INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:
                                            SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                         FSN                                             ITEM DESCRIPTION

      CL} 1005-672-1643                Machinegun, Cal. .30 Browning M1919A4, Flexible W/E.

PRESERVED:

                              (Month)  (Day)     .  (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                   Function                                               Mech  Insp

Re    a. Unit Responsibility.
      Assure that weapon and equipment are in good operating condition.
PON   Certify that weapon is free of ammunition in gun barrels, magazines and containers.
      Deliver complete weapon to designated processing area.
      Unit Commander or representative, will remain with equipment, until acceptance

          by responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                       WARNING

                  Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR. Do
                  not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to
                  insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in posi-
                  tion to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
                  work area.
      Disassemble weapon as necessary to accomplish desired cleaning. Remove handle
          assembly operating slide for packaging purposes.
      Clean all metallic surfaces by wiping with rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570)
          saturated with cleaning solvent (MIL—C-372) (6850-753-4806).
      Clean both barrel bores and chambers with swabs (1005-288-3565) saturated with
          bore cleaner (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806).
      Clean surfaces of parts having burned powder residue with a metallic bristle (1005—
          556-4174) saturated with bore cleaner (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod
          furnished as “‘special tools.”
      After cleaning with (MIL—C-372) (6850~-753-4806) rinse with a drycleaning solvent
          (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
      Drain and dry with compressed air or wiping rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570)
          changing drying position as necessary to accomplish thorough draining.
      Dry both barrels and chambers with swabs (11005-288-3565).
      All cleaned surfaces, of machinegun, shall be thoroughly dried prior to preservation.
      Preserve all metallic surfaces with preservation oil (VV—L-800) (9150-—281-—2007).
      Completely drain off excess oil prior to reassembly and packaging.
      Insert a bore tube volatile corrosion inhibitor (1005~-336-0225) into each barrel bore
          extending tube 1’’ beyond barrel end and crimp over.
      Wrap retracting slide handle assembly in paper volatile packaging (MIL-P-3420)
           (8135—-985-7242) size 3’’ x 5”.

TM 750-136

Step                                        Function                                        Mech  Insp

13 | Wrap a piece of paper volatile packaging around barrel jacket (MIL-P-3420) (8135-

        985-7242) 4’’ x 12’’ to locate wrapped handle assembly.

                                       APPENDIX A-21

             PREPARATION INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:
                           SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                         FSN                                              ITEM DESCRIPTION

      [] 1005-672-—1649                Machine Gun Caliber .30 191946 W/E.

PRESERVED:

                              (Month)  (Day)     (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF i180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

      Insp:  completed.

             For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                   Function                                                          Mech  Insp

moh   a. Unit Responsibility,                                                               containers.
                                                                                            acceptance
      Assure that weapon and equipment are in good operating condition.
      Certify that weapon is free of ammunition in gun barrels, magazines and
      Deliver complete weapon to designated processing area.
      Unit Commander or representative will remain with equipment until

          by responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                     WARNING

                 Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                  Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                 to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                 position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
                  work area.

non   Disassemble weapon as necessary to accomplish cleaning. Remove shoulder piece
          and carrying handle and package individually.
o o
      Clean all metallic surfaces by wiping with rag (DDD-—R-30) (7920-205-3570)
          saturated with cleaning solvent (MIL~C-372) (6850-753-4806).

      Clean both barrel bores and chambers with swabs (1005-288~3565) saturated with

          bore cleaner (MIL-—C-372) (6850-753-4806).
      Clean surfaces of parts having burned powder residue with a metallic brush (1005-

          556-4174) saturated with bore cleaner (MIL—C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod
          furnished as “‘special tools’.
      After cleaning with (MIL-—C-372) (6850-753-4806) rinse with a dry cleaning solvent
          (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
      Drain and dry with compressed air or wiping rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570)
          changing drying position as necessary to accomplish thorough draining.
      Dry both barrels and chambers with swabs (1005-288~3565).
      All cleaned surfaces of machine gun shall be thoroughly dried prior to preservation.
      Preserve all metallic surfaces with preservative oil (VV-L-800) (9150-281-2007).

      Completely drain off excess oil prior to reassembly and packaging.

                          FSN                            ITEM DESCRIPTION

      [} 1005-672~1767                  Submachine Gun, Cal. .45 M3, W/E.

PRESERVED:                     (Month)  (Day)                              (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                    Function                                               Mech  Insp

wb    a. Umt Responsibility.
      Assure that weapon and equipment are in operating condition prior to movement to

          processing site.
      Certify that weapon, magazines, and containers are free of ammunition.
      Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment, to designated processing site.
      Unit Commander, or representative will remain with weapons until acceptance by

          responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                      WARNING

             Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR. Do
             not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to
             insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in posi-
             tion to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
             work area.

Noe   Disassemble weapon, as necessary, prior to cleaning.
      Clean barrel bore, chamber, and any parts subjected to burned powder residues with

       a metallic brush (1005-550-4036), saturated with solvent cleaning compound.

       (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished as “special tools”.

      Further cleaning of all metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in

       solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and agitation, or,

          with solvent saturated rags (DDD-R~30) (7920-205-3570).          rags    (DDD-R-30)
      Rinse parts with clean solvent (P~D~680) (6850-285-8012).
      Drain, and dry clean parts with dry compressed air, or, with

          (7920-205-3570).

oo    The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried with swabs (1005-288-3565).
      Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials, and wood, shall be by any process,

          or combination of processes, which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
          damage to the item(s). Clean by brushing, wiping, etc.
      Reassemble weapon with the exception of the barrel, flash hider, magazine, and stock.
      Metallic surfaces of weapons shall be coated with corrosion preventative compound
          (VV-L-800) (9150-281-2007), applied by dipping.

        6. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.
  1 | Disassemble mount as necessary in preparation for cleaning.

            (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570) saturated with solvent cleaning compound
            (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806).

                              PREPARATION INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:
                                            SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                         FSN                    ITEM DESCRIPTION

      [J 1005-673-7955                 Pistol, Cal. .45, M1911, W/E.

PRESERVED:                             —_—                               en

                              (Month)        (Day)                    .  (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech:  For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                   Function                                               Mech  Insp

        a. Unit Responsibility.
 1 | Assure that weapon is in good operating condition.
 2 | Certify that weapon, magazines and containers are free of ammunition and explosives.
 3 | As directed, weapon, complete with tools and equipment will be delivered to desig-

            nated processing site.
 4 | Unit Commander, or representative, will remain with equipment until acceptance by

            responsible preservation site personnel.

        b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                          WARNING

                   Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR. Do
                    not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to
                   insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in posi-
                    tion to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of work

                              area.

 1 | Disassemble weapon, as necessary, to accomplish thorough cleaning.
 2 Clean barrel bore, chamber, and any parts having heavy contaminants with a metallic

            brush (1005-550-4036) saturated with solvent, cleaning compound (M1L-C-372)
             (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished as “special tools.”
 3 | Remove cleaner from bore and chamber using swabs, small arms, size 24’’ x 244’
              (1005~288-3565).
 4 | Further cleaning of all surfaces shall be accomplished by complete immersion in, or
           by wiping with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920~-205-3570) saturated with solvent, (P-D-
             680) (6850-285-8012) accompanied by scrubbing and agitation.
 5 | Position parts to allow complete drainage.
 6 | Rinse parts in clean solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012) to remove residues.
 7 | Drain and dry with compressed air, or wipe with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-
            3570).
 8 | Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials and wood, shall be by any process
             which will accomplish thorough cleaning without damage to the item(s). Clean by
             brushing, wiping, etc.
 9 | Reassemble weapon.
10 | Metallic surfaces of weapons shall be coated with corrosion preventative compound
             (VV-L-800) (9150-281-2007) applied by dipping.

                                                                                                    A-25-1


---

TM 750-136

Step                                     Function                                              Mech  Insp

11 | Drain excess oil from weapon changing draining position, as necessary, to accomplish
            thorough drainage.

12 | Wrap pistol with a 12’’ x 18”’ piece of paper, volatile (M1L—P-3420) (8135-985-
             7242).

13 | Place each wrapped pistol in a 10’’ x 12’’ bag (MIL—B-131) (8135~282-0565).
14 | Close bag by heat sealing.
15 | Place sealed bag in unit fiberboard box (PPP-F-320) (8135-132-9067) size, 10%’’ x

               6’ x 334’ r

16 | Clean magazines by immersion and agitation in solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
17 | Dry magazines with compressed air, or by wiping with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-

            205-3570).
18 | Overwrap magazine with paper, volatile (MIL—P-3420) (8135-985-7242) size,

                           6” x 9’.

19 | Clean holster by wiping with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920~-205-3570), or by using com-

        pressed air.

20 | Dry holster by wiping with rags (DDD~R-30) (7920-205-3570).

21 | Wrap all straps and thongs around body of holster.

22 | Wrap holster with barrier material (MIL—B-121) (8135--226-3124) size, 12’’ x 16’.

23 | Secure wrap with tape (PPP-T-60) (8135-890-9873) 2’’ x 4’’ (3 pieces required).

24 | Place wrapped magazine and holster in the unit container, see Step 16.

25 | Fill void space of unit container with cushioning material (PPP-C-843) (8135-855-

              6969) size, 12’’ x 12’’.

26 | Seal center seams of container with tape (PPP~T-60) (8135-890-9873) size 2’’ x

        13” (2 required).

27 | The following marking shall be applied to each unit container with ink, marking,

                                                           APPENDIX A-26

                                 PREPARATION INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:
                                               SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                            FSN                                      ITEM DESCRIPTION

      [] 1005-674—1309           Rifle, Caliber .30 Automatic Browning M1918, A2, W/E.

PRESERVED:

                                 (Month)  (Day)     .  (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech: For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                      Function                                            Mech  Insp

Ne    a, Unit Responsibility.
      Assure that the weapon is in operating condition prior to movement to processing site.
m W   Certify that weapon, magazine and container are free of ammunition.
      Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment, to designated processing site.
      Unit Commander or representative will remain with weapons until acceptance by

          responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                    WARNING

                  Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR. Do
                   not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to
                 insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in posi-
                  tion to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
                  work area.

      Disassemble weapon, as necessary, in preparation for cleaning. Remove bipod as-
          sembly and flash hider.

      Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with a cleaning rag (DDD-R~30) (7920-
          205-3570), saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL~C-372) (6850-
          753-4806).

      Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior metal surfaces with
          snug fitting bristle brushes, (1005-556-4174) and (1005-690-8441), saturated
          with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-—C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod fur-
          nished as “‘special tools”.

      Further cleaning of parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in dry-
          cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and
          agitation or with a solvent saturated wiping rag (DDD-R-30) (7920~205-3570).

      Rinse parts with clean dry-cleaning solvent (P-D~680) (6850-285-8012).
      Drain and dry cleaned parts with dry compressed air or with wiping rags (DDD-

          R-30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried with cleaning
          swabs, (1005-288-3565). All cleaned surfaces shall be dried prior to preservative
          application.
      Apply general purpose lubricating oil (VV-—L-800) (9150-281-2007), to all metallic
          surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be drained prior to packag-

            ing.

33 | Close bag by heat sealing.

             with volatile paper (MIL-—P-3420) (8135-985~7242) 20’’ x 36’’.

       Assure that weapon is in operating condition prior to movement to processing site.
Bm ON  Certify that weapon and containers are free of ammunition.
       Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment, to designated processing site.
a      Unit commander or representative will remain with weapons until acceptance by

           responsible preservation site personnel.

       b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                        WARNING

                   Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON 1S CLEAR.
                  Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                   to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                   position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
                   work area.

       Disassemble weapon as necessary in preparation for cleaning.
       Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with cleaning rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-

           3570), saturated with solvent cleaning compound (M]L-C-372) (6850-753-4806).
       Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior metal surfates with

           snug fitting bristle brushes (1005-556~4174 and 1005-690-8441), saturated with
           solvent cleaning compound (M1L-C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished
           as “special tools”.
       Further cleaning of parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in drycleaning
           solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and agitation or
           with a solvent-saturated cleaning rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570).
       Rinse parts with clean drycleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
       Drain and dry clean parts with dry compressed air or with wiping rags (DDD-R-
          30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried with cleaning
           swabs (1005-288-3565). All cleaned surfaces shall be dried prior to preservation
           application.
       Apply general purpose lubricating oil (VV—L-800) (9150-281-2007), to all metallic
           surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be drained prior to packaging.
       Reassemble weapon.
       Insert a VCI treated bore tube (1005-336-0225), into bore of barrel bending protrud-
           ing portion over the muzzle end.

      a. Unit Responsibility.

          responsible preservation site personnel.

     b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

                                                                    WARNING

                Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                 position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity
                  of work area.

1 | Disassemble weapon, as necessary, in preparation for thorough cleaning.
2} Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570),

         saturated with cleaning solvent compound (MIL-~C-372) (6850-753-4806).
3 | Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior surfaces with rags

         (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570), saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-
          C-372) (6850-753-4806).
4 | Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in
         dry-cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing
          and agitation or with a solvent saturated rag (DDD~R-30) (7920-205-3570).
5 | Rinse metal parts with clean dry-cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
6 | Drain and dry clean parts with dry compressed air or with wiping rags (DDD-R-
         30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried prior to pres-

          ervation and packaging, using swabs (1005-288-3565).
7 | Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials and wood shall be by any process

          or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without
           damage to the item(s) (brushing, wiping, etc.).

                          FSN                                      ITEM DESCRIPTION

      [1] 1005-678~-9828       Rifle, 7.62 Millimeter, M14 National Match W/E.

PRESERVED:

                               (Month)       (Day)      (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

      Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

      Mech:  For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

             completed.

      Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

             or processing personnel.

Step                                       Function                                           Mech  Insp

Ne    a. Unit Responsibility.
      Assure that weapon is in operating condition prior to movement to processing site.
B o   Certify that weapon, magazine and containers are free of ammunition.
      Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment to designated processing site.
      Unit Commander or representative will remain with weapons until accept anceby

          responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.

                                                                      WARNING
                  Before cleaning and packaging, Be Sure Weapon Is Clear. Do not actu-
                ‘ate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to insure
                  that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in position to
                  be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of work area.
      Disassemble weapon, as necessary, in preparation for thorough cleaning.

      Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570),

       saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806).

      Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior surfaces with snug

       fitting bristle brushes (1005-556-4174), and (1005-690-8441), saturated with

       solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished

       as “‘special tools’’.            ;

      Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in

       dry cleaning solvent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and

       agitation or with a solvent saturated rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570).

on    Rinse parts with clean dry-cleaning solvent (P~D-680) (6850-285-8012).

      Drain and dry clean parts with dry compressed air or with wiping rags (DDD-R-30)

       (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried prior to preservative

       application.

      Cleaning of canvas, webbing, synthetic materials, and wood shall be by any process

       or combination of processes which will accomplish thorough cleaning without

       damage to the item(s) (brushing, wiping, etc.).

      Apply general purpose lubricating oil (VV-L-800) (9150-281-2007), to all metallic

       surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be drained prior to packaging.

      Insert a VCI treated bore tube (1005-336-0225) into bore of barrel bending pro-

       truding portion over the muzzle end.

                     Before cleaning and packaging, Be Sure Weapon Is Clear. Do not

1 | Disassemble weapon as necessary in preparation for cleaning.
2 | Surfaces of the Back Plate Assembly with Solenoid shall be cleaned by any ap-

         plicable procedure. Contamination of the Solenoid with cleaning solvents or

         preservative oils shall be prevented.

3 | Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with a rag (DDD-—R-30) (7920-205-3570)

      ,  saturated with solvent cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806).

4 , Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants of exterior metal surfaces with

         snug fitting bristle brushes (1005-556-4174) and (1005-690-8441) saturated with

         solvent cleaning compound (MIL-—C-372) (6850-753-4806). Use rod furnished as

         “special tools’’.

5 | Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in dry
           cleaning solvent (P~D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and

         agitation or with a solvent saturated wiping rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570).

                                                        APPENDIX A-31
                              PREPARATION INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:

                                            SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                         FSN                                              ITEM DESCRIPTION

      [] 1005-694-4123                 Rifle, Caliber .22, Remington Model 40X H-1 (M12).

PRESERVED:                                        ee        Oo

                              (Month)             (Day)         (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:              Relates to instructions to be  accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.
        Function:  For initials of mechanic or    responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been
       Mech:       completed.

      Insp:        For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

                   or processing personnel.

Step                                              Function                                       Mech  Insp

brwH  a. Unit Responsibility.
      Assure that weapon is in operating condition prior to movement to processing site.
      Certify that weapon and containers are free of ammunition.
      Deliver weapon, complete with tools and equipment, to designated processing site.
      Unit Commander, or representative, will remain with weapons until acceptance by

         responsible preservation site personnel.

      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing and Marking.

                                                                     WARNING

                  Before cleaning and packaging, BE SURE WEAPON IS CLEAR.
                  Do not actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber
                  to insure that it is empty and check to see that no ammunition is in
                  position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
                  work area.

      Disassemble weapon as necessary in preparation for thorough cleaning.
      Clean exterior surfaces of metal parts with rags (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570)

          saturated with solvent, cleaning compound, (MIL—C-372) (6850-753-4806).
      Clean barrel bore, chamber and heavy contaminants, of exterior surfaces, with snug

          fitting bristle brush (1005-556-4174), and swabs (1005-288-3565), saturated with
          solvent, cleaning compound (MIL-C-372) (6850~753-4806). Use rod furnished as
          “special tools.” _
      Further cleaning of metal parts shall be accomplished by complete immersion in dry
          cleaning solvent, (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012), accompanied by scrubbing and
          agitation or with a solvent saturated cleaning rag (DDD-—R-30) (7920-205-3570).
      Rinse metal parts with clean dry-cleaning solvent, (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
      Drain and dry clean parts with dry, compressed air, or with wiping rags (DDD~R-
          30) (7920-205-3570). The barrel bore and chamber shall be dried prior to preserva-
          tive application.
      Apply general purpose lubricating oil (VV-L-800) (9150-281-2007) to all metallic
          surfaces by any applicable procedure. Excess oil shall be drained prior to packaging.
      Reassemble weapon.
      Insert a VCI treated bore tube (1005-336—0225) into bore of barrel, bending protrud-
          ing portion over the muzzle end.

                                                              APPENDIX A-34

                                    PREPARATION INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHIPMENT:
                                                  SURFACE VESSEL TRANSPORT

                               FSN                                                ITEM DESCRIPTION

           [] 1005-714-2393                  Machine Gun Cal. .30 Browning M1919 A4 Flexible.

PRESERVED:

                                    (Month)  (Day)     .  (Year)

PRESERVED FOR A MAXIMUM OF 180 DAYS

NOTE:

           Function: Relates to instructions to be accomplished in preparation of the equipment for shipment.

           Mech:  For initials of mechanic or responsible processing personnel to indicate function has been

                  completed.

           Insp:  For initials of inspector certifying that function has been completed as indicated by the mechanic

                  or processing personnel.

Step                                         Function                                               Mech  Insp

        a. Unit Responsibility.Ne
        Assure that weapon and equipment are in good operating condition.
        Certify that weapon is free of ammunition in gun barrels, magazines and containers.mw
        Deliver complete weapon to designated processing site.
        Unit Commander, or representative, will remain with equipment until acceptancefos)

            by responsible preservation site personnel.OI

ht      b. Cleaning, Preservation, Packaging, Packing, and Marking.me OO

                                                                        WARNING

                    Before cleaning and packaging, Be Sure Weapon Is Clear. Do not
                     actuate trigger until weapon has been cleared. Inspect chamber to
                     insure that it is emptn and check to see that no ammunition is in
                     position to be introduced. Avoid having live ammunition in vicinity of
                      work area.
        Disassemble weapon as necessary to accomplish desired cleaning. Remove handle
             assembly operating slide for packaging purposes.
        Clean all metallic surfaces by wiping with rag (DDD-R-~-30) (7920-205-3570)
             saturated with cleaning solvent (MIL~C-372) (6850~753-4806).
        Clean barrel bore and chamber with swabs (1005-288-3565) saturated with bore
            cleaner (MIL-C-372) (6850~753-4806).
        Clean surfaces of parts having burned powder residue with a metallic bristle brush
             (1005-556-4174) saturated with bore cleaner (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806).
             Use rod furnished as “‘special tools’.
        After cleaning with (MIL-C-372) (6850-753-4806) rinse with a dry cleaning sol-
              vent (P-D-680) (6850-285-8012).
        Drain and dry with compressed air or wiping rag (DDD-R-30) (7920-205-3570)
             changing drying position as necessary to accomplish thorough draining.
         Dry gun barrel and chambers with swabs (1005-288-3565).
        All surfaces, of machine gun, shall be thoroughly dried prior to preservation.
        Preserve all metallic surfaces with preservative oil (VV—L-800) (9150-281-2007).
         Completely drain off excess oil prior to reassembly and packaging.
         Insert a bore tube, volatile corrosion inhibitor (1005-336-0225) into barrel bore,
             extending tube one inch beyond barrel end and crimp over.
12 Wrap retracting slide handle assembly in paper, volatile packaging (MIL-P--3420)
             (8135-985--7242), size 3’’ x 5”,
